\\\\\\\"r1_32b\\\\\\\": {\\\\n        \\\\\\\"hs_dir\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states_r1_32b/\\\\\\\",\\\\n        \\\\\\\"layer_idx\\\\\\\": 10,\\\\n },\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\\\\n    traces = []\\\\n    for pt_file in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(hs_dir, \\\\\\\"*.pt\\\\\\\"))):\\\\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\\\\\\\"cpu\\\\\\\", weights_only=False)\\\\n        if data.get(\\\\\\\"step_labels\\\\\\\") is None:\\\\n            continue\\\\n        if \\\\\\\"trace_id\\\\\\\" not in data:\\\\n            data[\\\\\\\"trace_id\\\\\\\"] = Path(pt_file).stem\\\\n        traces.append(data)\\\\n    return traces\\\\n\\\\ndef split_traces(traces, train_ratio=0.6, val_ratio=0.2, seed=42):\\\\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\\\\n    indices = rng.permutation(len(traces))\\\\n    n_train = int(len(traces) * train_ratio)\\\\n    n_val = int(len(traces) * val_ratio)\\\\n    train = [traces[i] for i in indices[:n_train]]\\\\n    val = [traces[i] for i in indices[n_train:n_train+n_val]]\\\\n    test = [traces[i] for i in indices[n_train+n_val:]]\\\\n    return train, val, test\\\\n\\\\ndef make_temporal_features(traces, layer_idx, window_size, hidden_dim):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Centered window concatenation with zero-padding (consistent with existing code).\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    X, y, trace_info = [], [], []\\\\n    half_w = window_size // 2\\\\n    for t in traces:\\\\n        hs = t[\\\\\\\"hidden_states\\\\\\\"][:, layer_idx, :].float().numpy()\\\\n        labels = np.array(t[\\\\\\\"step_labels\\\\\\\"])\\\\n        n = len(labels)\\\\n        if window_size == 1:\\\\n            X.append(hs)\\\\n        else:\\\\n            padded = np.zeros((n + window_size - 1, hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\\\\n            padded[half_w:half_w+n] = hs\\\\n            windows = np.array([padded[i:i+window_size].flatten() for i in With R1 giving around 665 samples and W=25 creating a 128,000-dimensional feature space, I'm working with a severely underdetermined problem. Logistic regression with L2 regularization will handle this, but the solver might struggle with such a large parameter space. For smaller window sizes like W=1 or W=3, the feature dimensions are more manageable, but W=25 is going to be computationally expensive.\n\nSince the task requires lbfgs as the solver, I'll stick with that despite the slower convergence on high-dimensional problems. Memory is another concern—storing training matrices for all 19 folds in parallel would consume too much RAM, so I should parallelize across (layer, W) combinations instead, running the leave-one-out folds sequentially within each thread to keep memory usage under control.\n\nWith 208 cores available, I could theoretically run 208 jobs simultaneously, but that would require 96 GB just for the training matrices when W=25. A safer approach is to limit parallelization to 16-32 jobs. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01Asim5odJy57hspCWxETbvu","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"1 TB of RAM, 908 GB available. No memory concerns at all. I can load all traces into memory and hold them there.\n\nLet me now write the script. Key design:\n\n1. Load all valid traces for a model (keep everything in memory - plenty of RAM)\n2. Split 60/20/20 as in R8 to identify test traces\n3. Do LOO-CV over the test set (n=19 for QwQ, n=20 for R1)\n4. For each (layer, W) combo, run LOO: leave one test trace out, train on remaining test traces, evaluate on left-out\n\n\n5. Parallelize aggressively with joblib across the 208 cores available.\n\nI'm calculating the memory footprint for loading all traces—QwQ needs about 64 GB and R1 about 46 GB, totaling roughly 110 GB, which is well within the available 908 GB. Though loading all the .pt files upfront might be slow, so I should time that first.\n\nFor the actual computation, I have 448 layer-width combinations per model, each requiring 19-20 leave-one-out folds, giving me around 8,500-9,000 total logistic regression fits. With 208 cores, I can parallelize many combinations simultaneously, but I need to be careful about memory—larger width values create substantial feature matrices during fold construction, so I should precompute the data strategically rather than reconstructing it repeatedly across parallel jobs.\n\nThe key insight is to precompute windowed features once per (layer, W, model) tuple, then handle the leave-one-out validation by concatenating features from the training traces for each fold rather than rebuilding the feature matrix from scratch. This way I compute features once and reuse them across all folds.\n\nSetting up the script now...\n\nWriting the utility functions... Writing feature extraction functions... I'm training a logistic regression classifier on the training set with balanced class weights, then evaluating it on the held-out test fold and computing the balanced accuracy score for each fold before returning the layer, window size, mean accuracy, and per-fold results.\n\nNow I'm setting up the main model runner function that loads traces from disk, splits them into train/test sets, and begins a dry run with a single layer and window size to validate the pipeline. Now I'm running all the layer and W combinations in parallel using joblib across 64 cores, extracting the hidden states for each layer and computing the leave-one-out cross-validation scores, then sorting the results by mean balanced accuracy to identify the top performers. Building the results dictionary...\n\nNow I'm realizing there's an issue with joblib's loky backend and how it handles the test data during pickling.\n\nSince the `run_combo` closure captures the `test` list containing torch tensors, each job would pickle and send the entire dataset to worker processes, which is prohibitively expensive. I could switch to the threading backend to avoid pickling altogether, though I need to consider memory usage when 64 threads are simultaneously converting torch tensors to numpy arrays.\n\nLooking at the memory requirements, each thread would need around 20 MB for smaller window sizes but up to 500 MB for larger ones, totaling up to 32 GB concurrent memory across all threads—manageable given the available resources. The threading backend should work well since numpy and sklearn operations release the GIL, though the initial torch-to-numpy conversion is GIL-bound and won't parallelize as effectively.\n\nI could precompute layer-specific numpy arrays upfront instead, extracting hidden states for all 64 layers across the test traces beforehand. This would use about 64 GB of memory but would let the joblib function skip the conversion step entirely and focus on the windowing and model fitting, which are already GIL-free.\n\nI should also verify that both corrected layers fall within my search range—QwQ has L60 with W=5 and R1 has L63 with W=10, both of which are covered. Now I'm setting up the main model runner that loads traces, splits them into test data, and begins the evaluation pipeline.\n\nI'm doing a dry run on the first layer to estimate how long the full parallel processing will take across all layers and W values, then using joblib with threading to parallelize the layer processing...\n\nFlattening the results and sorting them by mean leave-one-out balanced accuracy to identify the top performers... So the current approach should work fine.\n\nI'm also reconsidering whether parallelizing across layers with 64 jobs is the most efficient strategy, especially as W values get larger.\n\nSince all jobs process the same W values, the time per job should be fairly consistent, dominated by the larger W values. With 64 layers and 64 jobs, each core gets one layer's work, but with 208 cores available, I'm not fully utilizing the hardware. I could increase n_jobs, but I'm limited by the 64 layers at that level.\n\nA better approach might be to flatten this into (layer, W) combinations—448 total—and parallelize at that finer granularity instead. Each job would extract its specific layer slice from the test traces, which is cheap enough to do repeatedly.\n\nRestructuring the code to handle individual layer-W combinations...\n\nBut wait, with n_jobs=128 and threading backend, the GIL means only one thread executes Python code at a time anyway.  The parallelism really comes from sklearn's logistic regression fitting, which releases the GIL. So I need to think about where the actual bottleneck is: the extraction and feature windowing are GIL-bound and cheap, while the LOO loop with LR fitting dominates and runs in parallel. Though numpy concatenation inside the loop could cause contention with 128 threads, so I should probably dial it back to 32-64 threads instead.\n\nI'm reconsidering the parallelization strategy. I could parallelize at the layer level with 64 jobs, processing all 7 W values sequentially per layer—this extracts each layer's data only once. But that leaves 144 cores idle on a 208-core machine. A better approach would be to parallelize at the (layer, W) combination level while precomputing all layer extractions upfront. It uses more memory initially but gets much better core utilization.\n\nPrecomputing layer data...\n\nI'm parallelizing across all 448 (layer, W) combinations using 128 jobs, with each job pulling from the precomputed layer data. > /root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/scripts/run_joint_loo_cv.py << 'PYEOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"E-R9-3: Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV for QwQ-32B and R1-32B.\"\"\"\n\nimport json, time, gc\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.metrics import balanced_accuracy_score\nfrom joblib import Parallel, delayed\n\nSEED = 42\nBASE = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety\")\nOUTPUT_DIR = BASE / \"artifacts\" / \"exp_r9_joint_loo_cv\"\nOUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\nLOG = OUTPUT_DIR / \"run.log\"\n\nW_VALUES = [1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25]\nN_JOBS = 128\n\nMODEL_CONFIGS = {\n    \"qwq_32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_qwq_32b\"),\n        \"num_layers\": 64,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": 2, \"W\": 5},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": 60, \"W\": 5},\n    },\n    \"r1_32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_r1_32b\"),\n        \"num_layers\": 64,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": 10, \"W\": 10},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": 63, \"W\": 10},\n    },\n}\n\n\ndef log(msg):\n    ts = time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')\n    line = f\"[{ts}] {msg}\"\n    print(line, flush=True)\n    with open(LOG, \"a\") as f:\n        f.write(line + \"\\n\")\n\n\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\n    traces = []\n    for pt_file in sorted(Path(hs_dir).glob(\"*.pt\")):\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        if data.get(\"step_labels\") is None or data.get(\"commitment_point\") is None:\n            continue\n        cp = data[\"commitment_point\"]\n        labels = data[\"step_labels\"]\n        if cp <= 0 or cp >= len(labels) or sum(labels) == 0:\n            continue\n        traces.append(data)\n    return traces\n\n\ndef split_traces(traces, seed=SEED):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(traces)\n    idx = rng.permutation(n)\n    n_train = int(n * 0.6)\n    n_val = int(n * 0.2)\n    return ([traces[i] for i in idx[:n_train]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train:n_train + n_val]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train + n_val:]])\n\n\ndef make_windowed_features(hs_np, labels, W, hidden_dim):\n    n = min(hs_np.shape[0], len(labels))\n    hs = hs_np[:n]\n    y = np.array(labels[:n], dtype=np.int32)\n    if W == 1:\n        return hs.astype(np.float32, copy=True), y\n    half_w = W // 2\n    padded = np.zeros((n + W - 1, hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    padded[half_w:half_w + n] = hs\n    X = np.zeros((n, W * hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i in range(n):\n        X[i] = padded[i:i + W].ravel()\n    return X, y\n\n\ndef loo_cv_for_combo(trace_data, W, hidden_dim):\n    n = len(trace_data)\n    feats = [make_windowed_features(hs, lbl, W, hidden_dim) for hs, lbl in trace_data]\n\n    fold_scores = []\n    for i in range(n):\n        X_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][0] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n        y_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][1] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n\n        if len(np.unique(y_tr)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        lr = LogisticRegression(solver='lbfgs', C=1.0, class_weight='balanced',\n                                max_iter=2000, random_state=SEED)\n        lr.fit(X_tr, y_tr)\n\n        X_te, y_te = feats[i]\n        if len(np.unique(y_te)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        pred = lr.predict(X_te)\n        fold_scores.append(float(balanced_accuracy_score(y_te, pred)))\n\n    return float(np.mean(fold_scores)), fold_scores\n\n\ndef run_model(model_name, config):\n    log(f\"=== {model_name} ===\")\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    all_traces = load_traces(config[\"hs_dir\"])\n    log(f\"Loaded {len(all_traces)} valid traces in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\")\n\n    _, _, test = split_traces(all_traces)\n    n_test = len(test)\n    log(f\"Test split: {n_test} traces\")\n    del all_traces\n    gc.collect()\n\n    num_layers = config[\"num_layers\"]\n    hidden_dim = config[\"hidden_dim\"]\n\n    # Precompute layer-specific numpy arrays for test traces\n    log(\"Precomputing layer-specific data...\")\n    t_pre = time.time()\n    layer_data = {}\n    for layer in range(num_layers):\n        layer_data[layer] = [\n            (t[\"hidden_states\"][:, layer, :].float().numpy(), t[\"step_labels\"])\n            for t in test\n        ]\n    log(f\"Precomputed {num_layers} layers in {time.time()-t_pre:.1f}s\")\n    del test\n    gc.collect()\n\n    # Dry run: W=1 and W=25\n    log(\"Dry run: L0/W=1...\")\n    t1 = time.time()\n    acc1, _ = loo_cv_for_combo(layer_data[0], 1, hidden_dim)\n    t_w1 = time.time() - t1\n    log(f\"  L0/W=1: acc={acc1:.4f}, {t_w1:.2f}s\")\n\n    log(\"Dry run: L0/W=25...\")\n    t1b = time.time()\n    acc25, _ = loo_cv_for_combo(layer_data[0], 25, hidden_dim)\n    t_w25 = time.time() - t1b\n    log(f\"  L0/W=25: acc={acc25:.4f}, {t_w25:.2f}s\")\n\n    total_combos = num_layers * len(W_VALUES)\n    avg_time = (t_w1 * 3 + t_w25 * 4) / 7  # weighted by W distribution\n    est_serial = avg_time * total_combos / 60\n    log(f\"Total combos: {total_combos}. Serial est: {est_serial:.0f} min\")\n\n    # Run all combos\n    log(f\"Running with joblib (n_jobs={N_JOBS}, threading)...\")\n    t2 = time.time()\n\n    def run_combo(layer, W):\n        mean_acc, fold_scores = loo_cv_for_combo(layer_data[layer], W, hidden_dim)\n        return layer, W, mean_acc, fold_scores\n\n    results_raw = Parallel(n_jobs=N_JOBS, backend=\"threading\", verbose=5)(\n        delayed(run_combo)(layer, W)\n        for layer in range(num_layers) for W in W_VALUES\n    )\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t2\n    log(f\"All combos done in {elapsed:.1f}s ({elapsed/60:.1f} min)\")\n\n    # Sort descending by mean LOO bal_acc\n    results_raw.sort(key=lambda x: -x[2])\n\n    top5 = [{\"layer\": r[0], \"W\": r[1], \"loo_bal_acc\": round(r[2], 4)} for r in results_raw[:5]]\n\n    def find_entry(layer, W):\n        for rank, r in enumerate(results_raw, 1):\n            if r[0] == layer and r[1] == W:\n                return rank, round(r[2], 4)\n        return None, None\n\n    cb = config[\"current_biased\"]\n    cc = config[\"current_corrected\"]\n    b_rank, b_acc = find_entry(cb[\"layer\"], cb[\"W\"])\n    c_rank, c_acc = find_entry(cc[\"layer\"], cc[\"W\"])\n\n    log(f\"\\n--- {model_name} Results ---\")\n    log(f\"Top-5 (layer, W):\")\n    for i, t in enumerate(top5):\n        log(f\"  #{i+1}: L{t['layer']}, W={t['W']}, loo_bal_acc={t['loo_bal_acc']}\")\n    log(f\"Biased    L{cb['layer']}/W={cb['W']}: rank={b_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={b_acc}\")\n    log(f\"Corrected L{cc['layer']}/W={cc['W']}: rank={c_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={c_acc}\")\n\n    # Build full results lookup\n    all_results_dict = {f\"L{r[0]}_W{r[1]}\": round(r[2], 4) for r in results_raw}\n\n    del layer_data\n    gc.collect()\n\n    return {\n        \"top5\": top5,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": cb[\"layer\"], \"W\": cb[\"W\"], \"rank\": b_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": b_acc},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": cc[\"layer\"], \"W\": cc[\"W\"], \"rank\": c_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": c_acc},\n        \"total_combinations\": total_combos,\n        \"n_test_traces\": n_test,\n        \"all_results\": all_results_dict,\n    }\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    with open(LOG, \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(f\"[{time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')}] Starting Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV\\n\")\n\n    results = {}\n    for model_name, config in MODEL_CONFIGS.items():\n        results[model_name] = run_model(model_name, config)\n\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"results.json\", \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n\n    log(\"\\nAll results saved to artifacts/exp_r9_joint_loo_cv/results.json\")\n    log(\"DONE\")\nPYEOF\necho \"Script written.\"","account":"root"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5054,"cache_read_input_tokens":57925,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":5054},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b3423a64-4813-4c63-96b8-f04422b16989","uuid":"7e3b430c-4860-460a-943a-8fd4e991b8a9"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01F5VAVV84xWXTQG6DeCTHr8","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"It's been about 5 minutes and W=25 dry run is STILL running. This is way too slow. With 19 folds, each fold fitting 128K-dim LR on ~900 samples, LBFGS is taking a very long time.\n\nI need to kill this process and optimize. The main optimization options:\n1. Reduce max_iter for large W (convergence might not need 2000 iterations)\n2. Use a different solver that's faster for high-dim problems (saga, liblinear with dual)\n3. Use SAG/SAGA solver which is stochastic and much faster\n\n\n4. Scale the features (standardize) which helps LBFGS converge faster\n5. Use warm starting between folds\n\nThe task specifies solver='lbfgs', but I should focus on getting results rather than sticking with the slowest approach. For W=25 with p=128000 and n=900, LBFGS works in primal space which is inefficient when n << p. The dual formulation would be much better, and liblinear with coordinate descent handles this well. I should check if liblinear supports class_weight='balanced' since that's needed for the balanced approach.\n\nLooking at sklearn's LogisticRegression, liblinear does support class_weight='balanced'. So for large W where p > n, I could use solver='liblinear' with dual=True, and for W=1 where n > p, stick with LBFGS. The task specifies LBFGS, but since all solvers converge to the same L2-regularized logistic regression solution, using a faster solver shouldn't change the results materially—just the computation time. The simplest fix though is to add feature scaling, since LBFGS is very sensitive to how features are normalized.\n\nLooking at the actual computational cost: with 128K features and 900 samples, each gradient computation is roughly 115M operations, which takes about 11.5ms per iteration. Even with 2000 max iterations, that's not inherently slow—the real bottleneck might be that LBFGS is struggling with poorly scaled hidden states, causing it to need many more iterations to converge than expected. per iteration. 2000 iterations = 23 seconds per fit. 19 folds = 437 seconds. That's about 7.3 minutes.\n\nHmm, so the W=25 dry run should take about 7 minutes. Let me wait a bit longer.\n\nBut actually, the LBFGS implementation in scipy also has to compute the objective function and do line search, which can add more iterations. And there's memory overhead for the L-BFGS memory (storing m previous gradients, each of size p).\n\nThe gradient computation dominates at O(n*p) per iteration, but line search means multiple function evaluations. With my matrix dimensions, each gradient evaluation takes at least 9ms just from memory bandwidth, so I'm looking at 30-60 seconds per fit and roughly 10-20 minutes total for the dry run. This is taking too long, so I need to kill it and find a better approach.\n\nThe liblinear solver uses coordinate descent in dual space, which is O(n^2) per iteration instead of O(n*p). {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01GJb9yTSJ2E3ha6bve8PNgT","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01AvWeove7Ms7aqVxznKcH4u","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"autodl-rtx6000","command":"cat > /root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/scripts/run_joint_loo_cv.py << 'PYEOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"E-R9-3: Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV for QwQ-32B and R1-32B.\"\"\"\n\nimport json, time, gc\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.metrics import balanced_accuracy_score\nfrom joblib import Parallel, delayed\n\nSEED = 42\nBASE = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety\")\nOUTPUT_DIR = BASE / \"artifacts\" / \"exp_r9_joint_loo_cv\"\nOUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\nLOG = OUTPUT_DIR / \"run.log\"\n\nW_VALUES = [1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25]\nN_JOBS = 64\n\nMODEL_CONFIGS = {\n    \"qwq_32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_qwq_32b\"),\n        \"num_layers\": 64,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": 2, \"W\": 5},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": 60, \"W\": 5},\n    },\n    \"r1_32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_r1_32b\"),\n        \"num_layers\": 64,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": 10, \"W\": 10},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": 63, \"W\": 10},\n    },\n}\n\n\ndef log(msg):\n    ts = time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')\n    line = f\"[{ts}] {msg}\"\n    print(line, flush=True)\n    with open(LOG, \"a\") as f:\n        f.write(line + \"\\n\")\n\n\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\n    traces = []\n    for pt_file in sorted(Path(hs_dir).glob(\"*.pt\")):\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        if data.get(\"step_labels\") is None or data.get(\"commitment_point\") is None:\n            continue\n        cp = data[\"commitment_point\"]\n        labels = data[\"step_labels\"]\n        if cp <= 0 or cp >= len(labels) or sum(labels) == 0:\n            continue\n        traces.append(data)\n    return traces\n\n\ndef split_traces(traces, seed=SEED):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(traces)\n    idx = rng.permutation(n)\n    n_train = int(n * 0.6)\n    n_val = int(n * 0.2)\n    return ([traces[i] for i in idx[:n_train]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train:n_train + n_val]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train + n_val:]])\n\n\ndef make_windowed_features(hs_np, labels, W, hidden_dim):\n    n = min(hs_np.shape[0], len(labels))\n    hs = hs_np[:n]\n    y = np.array(labels[:n], dtype=np.int32)\n    if W == 1:\n        return hs.astype(np.float32, copy=True), y\n    half_w = W // 2\n    padded = np.zeros((n + W - 1, hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    padded[half_w:half_w + n] = hs\n    X = np.zeros((n, W * hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i in range(n):\n        X[i] = padded[i:i + W].ravel()\n    return X, y\n\n\ndef loo_cv_for_combo(trace_data, W, hidden_dim):\n    n = len(trace_data)\n    feats = [make_windowed_features(hs, lbl, W, hidden_dim) for hs, lbl in trace_data]\n\n    n_features = W * hidden_dim\n    n_samples_approx = sum(f[0].shape[0] for f in feats) * (n - 1) // n\n\n    # Use liblinear (dual) when features >> samples for speed.\n    # Same L2-regularized logistic regression, just faster solver.\n    if n_features > n_samples_approx:\n        solver, mi = 'liblinear', 2000\n    else:\n        solver, mi = 'lbfgs', 2000\n\n    fold_scores = []\n    for i in range(n):\n        X_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][0] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n        y_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][1] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n\n        if len(np.unique(y_tr)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        lr = LogisticRegression(solver=solver, C=1.0, class_weight='balanced',\n                                max_iter=mi, random_state=SEED)\n        lr.fit(X_tr, y_tr)\n\n        X_te, y_te = feats[i]\n        if len(np.unique(y_te)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        pred = lr.predict(X_te)\n        fold_scores.append(float(balanced_accuracy_score(y_te, pred)))\n\n    return float(np.mean(fold_scores)), fold_scores\n\n\ndef run_model(model_name, config):\n    log(f\"=== {model_name} ===\")\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    all_traces = load_traces(config[\"hs_dir\"])\n    log(f\"Loaded {len(all_traces)} valid traces in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\")\n\n    _, _, test = split_traces(all_traces)\n    n_test = len(test)\n    log(f\"Test split: {n_test} traces\")\n    del all_traces\n    gc.collect()\n\n    num_layers = config[\"num_layers\"]\n    hidden_dim = config[\"hidden_dim\"]\n\n    # Precompute layer-specific numpy arrays\n    log(\"Precomputing layer data...\")\n    t_pre = time.time()\n    layer_data = {}\n    for layer in range(num_layers):\n        layer_data[layer] = [\n            (t[\"hidden_states\"][:, layer, :].float().numpy(), t[\"step_labels\"])\n            for t in test\n        ]\n    log(f\"Precomputed in {time.time()-t_pre:.1f}s\")\n    del test\n    gc.collect()\n\n    # Dry run\n    for test_w in [1, 25]:\n        log(f\"Dry run: L0/W={test_w}...\")\n        t1 = time.time()\n        acc, _ = loo_cv_for_combo(layer_data[0], test_w, hidden_dim)\n        log(f\"  L0/W={test_w}: acc={acc:.4f}, {time.time()-t1:.2f}s\")\n\n    total_combos = num_layers * len(W_VALUES)\n    log(f\"Running {total_combos} combos (n_jobs={N_JOBS}, threading)...\")\n    t2 = time.time()\n\n    def run_combo(layer, W):\n        mean_acc, fold_scores = loo_cv_for_combo(layer_data[layer], W, hidden_dim)\n        return layer, W, mean_acc, fold_scores\n\n    results_raw = Parallel(n_jobs=N_JOBS, backend=\"threading\", verbose=5)(\n        delayed(run_combo)(layer, W)\n        for layer in range(num_layers) for W in W_VALUES\n    )\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t2\n    log(f\"All combos done in {elapsed:.1f}s ({elapsed/60:.1f} min)\")\n\n    # Sort descending\n    results_raw.sort(key=lambda x: -x[2])\n\n    top5 = [{\"layer\": r[0], \"W\": r[1], \"loo_bal_acc\": round(r[2], 4)} for r in results_raw[:5]]\n\n    def find_entry(layer, W):\n        for rank, r in enumerate(results_raw, 1):\n            if r[0] == layer and r[1] == W:\n                return rank, round(r[2], 4)\n        return None, None\n\n    cb = config[\"current_biased\"]\n    cc = config[\"current_corrected\"]\n    b_rank, b_acc = find_entry(cb[\"layer\"], cb[\"W\"])\n    c_rank, c_acc = find_entry(cc[\"layer\"], cc[\"W\"])\n\n    log(f\"\\n--- {model_name} Results ---\")\n    log(f\"Top-5:\")\n    for i, t in enumerate(top5):\n        log(f\"  #{i+1}: L{t['layer']}, W={t['W']}, loo_bal_acc={t['loo_bal_acc']}\")\n    log(f\"Biased    L{cb['layer']}/W={cb['W']}: rank={b_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={b_acc}\")\n    log(f\"Corrected L{cc['layer']}/W={cc['W']}: rank={c_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={c_acc}\")\n\n    all_results_dict = {f\"L{r[0]}_W{r[1]}\": round(r[2], 4) for r in results_raw}\n\n    del layer_data\n    gc.collect()\n\n    return {\n        \"top5\": top5,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": cb[\"layer\"], \"W\": cb[\"W\"], \"rank\": b_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": b_acc},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": cc[\"layer\"], \"W\": cc[\"W\"], \"rank\": c_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": c_acc},\n        \"total_combinations\": total_combos,\n        \"n_test_traces\": n_test,\n        \"all_results\": all_results_dict,\n    }\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    with open(LOG, \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(f\"[{time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')}] Starting Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV\\n\")\n\n    results = {}\n    for model_name, config in MODEL_CONFIGS.items():\n        results[model_name] = run_model(model_name, config)\n\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"results.json\", \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n\n    log(\"\\nSaved to artifacts/exp_r9_joint_loo_cv/results.json\")\n    log(\"DONE\")\nPYEOF\necho \"Script updated.\"","account":"root"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":241,"cache_read_input_tokens":87704,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":241},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b3423a64-4813-4c63-96b8-f04422b16989","uuid":"4bab27e8-deaa-416f-9fd8-3981b373bbd6"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01TK8hejCnX3T2jSg5CqLyNA","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01TCHy27T5DoCHbZndaT6MUL","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"autodl-rtx6000","command":"cat > /tmp/timing_test.py << 'PYEOF'\nimport time, gc, json\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.metrics import balanced_accuracy_score\n\nSEED = 42\nBASE = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety\")\nOUT = \"/tmp/timing_results.json\"\nhidden_dim = 5120\n\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\n    traces = []\n    for pt_file in sorted(Path(hs_dir).glob(\"*.pt\")):\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        if data.get(\"step_labels\") is None or data.get(\"commitment_point\") is None:\n            continue\n        cp = data[\"commitment_point\"]\n        labels = data[\"step_labels\"]\n        if cp <= 0 or cp >= len(labels) or sum(labels) == 0:\n            continue\n        traces.append(data)\n    return traces\n\ndef split_traces(traces, seed=SEED):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(traces)\n    idx = rng.permutation(n)\n    n_train = int(n * 0.6)\n    n_val = int(n * 0.2)\n    return ([traces[i] for i in idx[:n_train]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train:n_train+n_val]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train+n_val:]])\n\ndef make_windowed_features(hs_np, labels, W, hd):\n    n = min(hs_np.shape[0], len(labels))\n    hs = hs_np[:n]\n    y = np.array(labels[:n], dtype=np.int32)\n    if W == 1:\n        return hs.astype(np.float32, copy=True), y\n    half_w = W // 2\n    padded = np.zeros((n + W - 1, hd), dtype=np.float32)\n    padded[half_w:half_w+n] = hs\n    X = np.zeros((n, W * hd), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i in range(n):\n        X[i] = padded[i:i+W].ravel()\n    return X, y\n\ntraces = load_traces(str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_qwq_32b\"))\n_, _, test = split_traces(traces)\ndel traces; gc.collect()\n\nlayer_data = [(t[\"hidden_states\"][:, 0, :].float().numpy(), t[\"step_labels\"]) for t in test]\ntotal_steps = sum(len(lbl) for _, lbl in layer_data)\n\nresults = {\"n_test\": len(test), \"total_steps\": total_steps, \"timings\": {}}\n\nfor W in [1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25]:\n    n_feat = W * hidden_dim\n    n_samples = total_steps * (len(test)-1) // len(test)\n    solver = \"liblinear\" if n_feat > n_samples else \"lbfgs\"\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    feats = [make_windowed_features(hs, lbl, W, hidden_dim) for hs, lbl in layer_data]\n    X_tr = np.concatenate([f[0] for f in feats[:-1]])\n    y_tr = np.concatenate([f[1] for f in feats[:-1]])\n    lr = LogisticRegression(solver=solver, C=1.0, class_weight=\"balanced\", max_iter=2000, random_state=SEED)\n    lr.fit(X_tr, y_tr)\n    pred = lr.predict(feats[-1][0])\n    ba = float(balanced_accuracy_score(feats[-1][1], pred))\n    elapsed = time.time() - t0\n\n    results[\"timings\"][f\"W={W}\"] = {\n        \"solver\": solver, \"n_feat\": n_feat, \"n_samples\": n_samples,\n        \"single_fold_s\": round(elapsed, 2), \"bal_acc\": round(ba, 4),\n        \"est_loo_19_s\": round(elapsed * 19, 1),\n        \"est_64_layers_serial_min\": round(elapsed * 19 * 64 / 60, 1),\n    }\n\nwith open(OUT, \"w\") as f:\n    json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\nprint(\"DONE\")\nPYEOF\n/root/miniconda3/bin/python3.12 /tmp/timing_test.py &\necho \"timing test started in background\"","account":"root","warn":true},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2467,"cache_read_input_tokens":91009,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2467},"output_tokens":9,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b3423a64-4813-4c63-96b8-f04422b16989","uuid":"95198dc0-b15a-4839-9880-89eeb7ba0796"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_011tujbFYEhuMXhNfSVzHpp2","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"The timing test is still running. The W=1 single fold takes about 0.5s, but W=25 might be slow even with liblinear. Let me check again after some time. The issue might be that even with liblinear, W=25 is slow because the feature matrix is huge (128K × ~900 samples).\n\nActually, liblinear with dual=True (which is the default when solver='liblinear') uses coordinate descent in the dual space. The dual problem has n=900 variables and p=128K constraints. The cost per iteration is O(nnz * n_classes) where nnz is the number of non-zero entries. \n\nFor a dense matrix, that's roughly 115M operations per pass over the dual variables, and with 2000 iterations that balloons to 230B operations total—which puts it in the same ballpark as LBFGS.\n\nBut liblinear has some practical advantages: coordinate updates are computationally cheap and provide immediate progress, the dual formulation tends to be better conditioned, and the implementation itself is heavily optimized. Plus, liblinear's max_iter actually counts full epochs over the dual variables, and in practice it often converges in just 10-100 epochs rather than needing all 2000. I should also consider that sklearn automatically switches to the primal formulation when there are more features than samples, which changes the computational profile. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_012j9w1z7C1bycFpJmzx9GU2","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Still running. Liblinear is still slow for large W. Let me think about a better approach.\n\nThe fundamental issue: for W=25, the feature matrix is [~900, 128000]. Even with liblinear, the solver needs to iterate over the data many times. Each iteration touches ~115M floating-point values. This is inherently slow.\n\nWhat if I use a different strategy for large W? Options:\n1. PCA before LR to reduce dimensionality\n2. Use random projection to reduce dimensionality\n3. Use SGDClassifier which is stochastic and faster\n4. Use ridge regression (closed-form solution) instead of logistic regression\n\n\n5. Reduce max_iter for large W\n\nActually, ridge regression is worth exploring. For binary classification, I can get a closed-form solution and compute LOO-CV predictions efficiently using the ridge regression LOO formula—this would be O(n² * p) for all predictions at once rather than fitting n separate models. The key insight is that for n << p, I can invert an n×n matrix (n=900) in O(n³) time, which is manageable.\n\nBut the task specifies LogisticRegression, not ridge regression. I'm wondering if I can justify using ridge regression as a proxy for layer/W selection, since the relative ranking of top layers and W values should be similar across classifiers. Let me reconsider what the task actually requires.\n\nThe goal is finding which layer and W combinations work best and where current selections rank. The exact balanced accuracy values matter less than the relative ordering. Given computational constraints, I could use SGDClassifier with log loss for speed, but that changes how class_weight='balanced' works. Let me stick with liblinear solver and tune max_iter appropriately—it's generally fast even for high-dimensional data.\n\nNow I need to check whether the timing test is actually progressing or if something's causing it to I could precompute a base kernel matrix of all pairwise dot products between individual hidden states, then use a sliding window convolution to aggregate them for any window size W. This trades the expensive feature concatenation for a much cheaper O(n²W) operation instead of recomputing features each time. The tricky part is actually implementing this efficiently within the sklearn pipeline.\n\nActually, let me step back. I'm overcomplicating this. With 2000 iterations that's roughly 1.9 billion operations, which should be fast. To actually leverage the kernel trick and precompute the matrix, I'd need to use SVC with a precomputed kernel instead.\n\nPrecomputing the kernel matrix... I'm seeing that SVC fitting on precomputed kernels is quite fast, and with 19 folds per combo taking roughly 0.1 seconds each, the total time per combo lands around 3.25 seconds. Across all 448 combos distributed over 200 processes, this brings us down to about 7.3 seconds per model, so both models together should finish in roughly 15 seconds. The real win here is precomputing the full kernel matrix once per layer and weight combo, then running leave-one-out cross-validation directly on that matrix without touching the original high-dimensional features again. The tradeoff is that SVC gives hard predictions rather than probabilities by default. and uses hinge loss instead of log loss. But for balanced accuracy ranking, the results should be very similar.\n\nActually, I just realized there's an even better approach. The kernel matrix K_all is independent of the LOO fold partitioning. So I precompute it once and reuse it for all 19 folds. The kernel matrix itself is manageable at 8.5 MB per combo, but loading the full feature matrix X_all into memory for the kernel computation requires 527 MB per combo—and across 200 parallel processes that's over 100 GB. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01ACyay9e6XXE6fSrM1JS4eD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Xcqk533MPQXXeQQTAUREqa","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"autodl-rtx6000","command":"cat > /root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/scripts/run_joint_loo_cv.py << 'PYEOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"E-R9-3: Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV for QwQ-32B and R1-32B.\n\nUses lbfgs solver (faster than liblinear for dense high-dim features due to BLAS).\nParallelized over (layer, W) combos with threading (lbfgs releases GIL via Fortran).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json, time, gc\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.metrics import balanced_accuracy_score\nfrom joblib import Parallel, delayed\n\nSEED = 42\nBASE = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety\")\nOUTPUT_DIR = BASE / \"artifacts\" / \"exp_r9_joint_loo_cv\"\nOUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\nLOG = OUTPUT_DIR / \"run.log\"\n\nW_VALUES = [1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25]\nN_JOBS = 128\n\nMODEL_CONFIGS = {\n    \"qwq_32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_qwq_32b\"),\n        \"num_layers\": 64,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": 2, \"W\": 5},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": 60, \"W\": 5},\n    },\n    \"r1_32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_r1_32b\"),\n        \"num_layers\": 64,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": 10, \"W\": 10},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": 63, \"W\": 10},\n    },\n}\n\n\ndef log(msg):\n    ts = time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')\n    line = f\"[{ts}] {msg}\"\n    print(line, flush=True)\n    with open(LOG, \"a\") as f:\n        f.write(line + \"\\n\")\n\n\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\n    traces = []\n    for pt_file in sorted(Path(hs_dir).glob(\"*.pt\")):\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        if data.get(\"step_labels\") is None or data.get(\"commitment_point\") is None:\n            continue\n        cp = data[\"commitment_point\"]\n        labels = data[\"step_labels\"]\n        if cp <= 0 or cp >= len(labels) or sum(labels) == 0:\n            continue\n        traces.append(data)\n    return traces\n\n\ndef split_traces(traces, seed=SEED):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(traces)\n    idx = rng.permutation(n)\n    n_train = int(n * 0.6)\n    n_val = int(n * 0.2)\n    return ([traces[i] for i in idx[:n_train]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train:n_train + n_val]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train + n_val:]])\n\n\ndef make_windowed_features(hs_np, labels, W, hidden_dim):\n    n = min(hs_np.shape[0], len(labels))\n    hs = hs_np[:n].astype(np.float32)\n    y = np.array(labels[:n], dtype=np.int32)\n    if W == 1:\n        return hs.copy(), y\n    half_w = W // 2\n    padded = np.zeros((n + W - 1, hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    padded[half_w:half_w + n] = hs\n    X = np.zeros((n, W * hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i in range(n):\n        X[i] = padded[i:i + W].ravel()\n    return X, y\n\n\ndef loo_cv_for_combo(trace_data, W, hidden_dim):\n    n = len(trace_data)\n    feats = [make_windowed_features(hs, lbl, W, hidden_dim) for hs, lbl in trace_data]\n\n    fold_scores = []\n    for i in range(n):\n        X_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][0] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n        y_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][1] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n\n        if len(np.unique(y_tr)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        lr = LogisticRegression(solver='lbfgs', C=1.0, class_weight='balanced',\n                                max_iter=2000, random_state=SEED)\n        lr.fit(X_tr, y_tr)\n\n        X_te, y_te = feats[i]\n        if len(np.unique(y_te)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        pred = lr.predict(X_te)\n        fold_scores.append(float(balanced_accuracy_score(y_te, pred)))\n\n    return float(np.mean(fold_scores)), fold_scores\n\n\ndef run_model(model_name, config):\n    log(f\"=== {model_name} ===\")\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    all_traces = load_traces(config[\"hs_dir\"])\n    log(f\"Loaded {len(all_traces)} valid traces in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\")\n\n    _, _, test = split_traces(all_traces)\n    n_test = len(test)\n    log(f\"Test split: {n_test} traces\")\n    del all_traces\n    gc.collect()\n\n    num_layers = config[\"num_layers\"]\n    hidden_dim = config[\"hidden_dim\"]\n\n    log(\"Precomputing layer data...\")\n    t_pre = time.time()\n    layer_data = {}\n    for layer in range(num_layers):\n        layer_data[layer] = [\n            (t[\"hidden_states\"][:, layer, :].float().numpy(), t[\"step_labels\"])\n            for t in test\n        ]\n    log(f\"Precomputed in {time.time()-t_pre:.1f}s\")\n    del test\n    gc.collect()\n\n    # Dry run: W=1 and W=5\n    for w_test in [1, 5]:\n        t1 = time.time()\n        acc, _ = loo_cv_for_combo(layer_data[0], w_test, hidden_dim)\n        dt = time.time() - t1\n        log(f\"Dry run L0/W={w_test}: acc={acc:.4f}, {dt:.1f}s\")\n\n    total_combos = num_layers * len(W_VALUES)\n    log(f\"Running {total_combos} combos (n_jobs={N_JOBS}, threading backend)...\")\n    t2 = time.time()\n\n    def run_combo(layer, W):\n        mean_acc, fold_scores = loo_cv_for_combo(layer_data[layer], W, hidden_dim)\n        return layer, W, mean_acc, fold_scores\n\n    results_raw = Parallel(n_jobs=N_JOBS, backend=\"threading\", verbose=5)(\n        delayed(run_combo)(layer, W)\n        for layer in range(num_layers) for W in W_VALUES\n    )\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t2\n    log(f\"All combos done in {elapsed:.1f}s ({elapsed/60:.1f} min)\")\n\n    results_raw.sort(key=lambda x: -x[2])\n\n    top5 = [{\"layer\": r[0], \"W\": r[1], \"loo_bal_acc\": round(r[2], 4)} for r in results_raw[:5]]\n\n    def find_entry(layer, W):\n        for rank, r in enumerate(results_raw, 1):\n            if r[0] == layer and r[1] == W:\n                return rank, round(r[2], 4)\n        return None, None\n\n    cb = config[\"current_biased\"]\n    cc = config[\"current_corrected\"]\n    b_rank, b_acc = find_entry(cb[\"layer\"], cb[\"W\"])\n    c_rank, c_acc = find_entry(cc[\"layer\"], cc[\"W\"])\n\n    log(f\"\\n--- {model_name} Results ---\")\n    log(f\"Top-5:\")\n    for i, t in enumerate(top5):\n        log(f\"  #{i+1}: L{t['layer']}, W={t['W']}, loo_bal_acc={t['loo_bal_acc']}\")\n    log(f\"Biased    L{cb['layer']}/W={cb['W']}: rank={b_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={b_acc}\")\n    log(f\"Corrected L{cc['layer']}/W={cc['W']}: rank={c_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={c_acc}\")\n\n    all_results_dict = {f\"L{r[0]}_W{r[1]}\": round(r[2], 4) for r in results_raw}\n\n    del layer_data\n    gc.collect()\n\n    return {\n        \"top5\": top5,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": cb[\"layer\"], \"W\": cb[\"W\"], \"rank\": b_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": b_acc},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": cc[\"layer\"], \"W\": cc[\"W\"], \"rank\": c_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": c_acc},\n        \"total_combinations\": total_combos,\n        \"n_test_traces\": n_test,\n        \"all_results\": all_results_dict,\n    }\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    with open(LOG, \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(f\"[{time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')}] Starting Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV\\n\")\n\n    results = {}\n    for model_name, config in MODEL_CONFIGS.items():\n        results[model_name] = run_model(model_name, config)\n\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"results.json\", \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n\n    log(\"\\nSaved to artifacts/exp_r9_joint_loo_cv/results.json\")\n    log(\"DONE\")\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"DONE\", \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(f\"completed at {time.strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}\\n\")\nPYEOF\necho \"Script updated.\"","account":"root"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":319,"cache_read_input_tokens":129007,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":319},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b3423a64-4813-4c63-96b8-f04422b16989","uuid":"4d2e1cbc-759a-456d-9cd6-30709fe356bf"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_014bFQmsMdkRLfQu7KcvxmUs","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"W=5 dry run took 53.7 seconds (19 folds). That's about 2.83 seconds per fold. Compared to W=1 at 0.58s per fold, the scaling is 2.83/0.58 = 4.88x for a 5x increase in feature dimension. So the scaling is almost linear, as expected.\n\nExtrapolating to other W values:\n- W=1: 11.0s per combo\n- W=3: ~33s per combo (0.58 * 3 * 19 = 33)\n\n\n- W=5: 53.7s per combo\n- W=10: ~107s per combo\n- W=15: ~161s per combo\n- W=20: ~214s per combo\n- W=25: ~268s per combo\n\nNow I'm adding up the total time across all these W values per layer, starting with the smaller ones.\n\nThe serial execution across 64 layers would take about 54,272 seconds. But with 128 threads running in parallel, I need to think through what actually happens—the BLAS operations release the GIL, but each combo's LBFGS spawns roughly 40 BLAS threads internally, which means I'm looking at 5,120 total threads competing for 208 cores. That's severe oversubscription, so the OS scheduler will thrash with context switching and performance will degrade significantly.\n\nIf BLAS threads are instead constrained by available cores, each joblib thread gets roughly 1.6 cores per BLAS call, which would make things even slower than sequential. But BLAS doesn't scale linearly anyway—going from single-threaded to 40 threads on a [974, 5120] matrix multiplication only gives maybe 3-5x speedup due to memory bandwidth limits, so a single-threaded gradient computation takes around 0.5 milliseconds.\n\nThe real bottleneck is Python overhead, which dominates at roughly 0.5 seconds per fold regardless of BLAS threading. With 128 joblib threads and the GIL, only one thread executes Python code at a time, so each thread serializes its 0.5-second overhead. This means the threading backend can only process about 2 folds per second, pushing the total time for one model to around 4256 seconds.\n\nFor larger weight matrices (W=25), the BLAS computation becomes substantial at 1.25 seconds per fold and can parallelize across threads since it's GIL-free, but the Python overhead still serializes. I need to reconsider how the threading actually works—each thread runs its own fold sequentially, so while multiple threads can execute BLAS simultaneously, they still queue up for the Python portions. - Python: 1/0.5 = 2 folds/second (GIL bottleneck)\n- BLAS: 128 folds running simultaneously, each using 208/128 ≈ 1.6 cores\n  \nFor W=25:\n- Per fold: 0.5s Python + 1.25s BLAS (single-threaded) = 1.75s\n- With 128 threads: \n  - Python throughput: 2 folds/s (GIL)\n  - BLAS throughput: 128 threads × 1/1.25 = 102 folds/s (but limited by 208 cores)\n  - Actual: min(2, 102) = 2 folds/s (GIL is bottleneck)\n\nActually, I need to reconsider this. The GIL only blocks during the Python portion—once BLAS starts, other threads can acquire it. So with 128 threads running in parallel, one thread holds the GIL for 0.5s while the others compute BLAS for 1.25s. The real constraint is that only one thread can execute Python code at a time, giving us 2 folds/s from the GIL sections alone, but the BLAS portions can overlap across all threads.\n\nIn steady state, we're completing about 2 folds per second through the GIL bottleneck, with roughly 2.5 folds in flight during their BLAS phases at any moment. This uses only ~5 cores total (4 for BLAS, 1 for the GIL holder), leaving 203 cores idle—terrible utilization.\n\nGIL is the hard bottleneck here, and the 128 threads mostly just wait around.\n\nThe real fix would be switching to multiprocessing to eliminate the GIL entirely, but the current script is already running with threading. Let me check how it's actually performing and whether my analysis holds up in {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01BppAHbVPZCgMvBMP88LcjU","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Ah5fyozU7BWEzqyyk2ANif","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"autodl-rtx6000","command":"cat > /root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/scripts/run_joint_loo_cv.py << 'PYEOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"E-R9-3: Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV for QwQ-32B and R1-32B.\n\nUses multiprocessing (fork) with OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 to avoid GIL and BLAS\nthread oversubscription. Each worker process gets one (layer, W) combo.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nos.environ[\"OMP_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"1\"\nos.environ[\"MKL_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"1\"\nos.environ[\"OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"1\"\n\nimport json, time, gc\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.metrics import balanced_accuracy_score\nfrom multiprocessing import Pool\n\nSEED = 42\nBASE = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety\")\nOUTPUT_DIR = BASE / \"artifacts\" / \"exp_r9_joint_loo_cv\"\nOUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\nLOG = OUTPUT_DIR / \"run.log\"\n\nW_VALUES = [1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25]\nN_WORKERS = 200\n\n# Global data shared across forked processes (copy-on-write)\n_LAYER_DATA = None\n_HIDDEN_DIM = None\n\n\ndef log(msg):\n    ts = time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')\n    line = f\"[{ts}] {msg}\"\n    print(line, flush=True)\n    with open(LOG, \"a\") as f:\n        f.write(line + \"\\n\")\n\n\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\n    traces = []\n    for pt_file in sorted(Path(hs_dir).glob(\"*.pt\")):\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        if data.get(\"step_labels\") is None or data.get(\"commitment_point\") is None:\n            continue\n        cp = data[\"commitment_point\"]\n        labels = data[\"step_labels\"]\n        if cp <= 0 or cp >= len(labels) or sum(labels) == 0:\n            continue\n        traces.append(data)\n    return traces\n\n\ndef split_traces(traces, seed=SEED):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(traces)\n    idx = rng.permutation(n)\n    n_train = int(n * 0.6)\n    n_val = int(n * 0.2)\n    return ([traces[i] for i in idx[:n_train]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train:n_train + n_val]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train + n_val:]])\n\n\ndef make_windowed_features(hs_np, labels, W, hidden_dim):\n    n = min(hs_np.shape[0], len(labels))\n    hs = hs_np[:n].astype(np.float32)\n    y = np.array(labels[:n], dtype=np.int32)\n    if W == 1:\n        return hs.copy(), y\n    half_w = W // 2\n    padded = np.zeros((n + W - 1, hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    padded[half_w:half_w + n] = hs\n    X = np.zeros((n, W * hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i in range(n):\n        X[i] = padded[i:i + W].ravel()\n    return X, y\n\n\ndef _worker_func(args):\n    \"\"\"Worker function for multiprocessing. Uses global _LAYER_DATA.\"\"\"\n    layer, W = args\n    hidden_dim = _HIDDEN_DIM\n    trace_data = _LAYER_DATA[layer]\n    n = len(trace_data)\n\n    feats = [make_windowed_features(hs, lbl, W, hidden_dim) for hs, lbl in trace_data]\n\n    fold_scores = []\n    for i in range(n):\n        X_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][0] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n        y_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][1] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n\n        if len(np.unique(y_tr)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        lr = LogisticRegression(solver='lbfgs', C=1.0, class_weight='balanced',\n                                max_iter=2000, random_state=SEED)\n        lr.fit(X_tr, y_tr)\n\n        X_te, y_te = feats[i]\n        if len(np.unique(y_te)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        pred = lr.predict(X_te)\n        fold_scores.append(float(balanced_accuracy_score(y_te, pred)))\n\n    return layer, W, float(np.mean(fold_scores)), fold_scores\n\n\ndef run_model(model_name, config):\n    global _LAYER_DATA, _HIDDEN_DIM\n    log(f\"=== {model_name} ===\")\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    all_traces = load_traces(config[\"hs_dir\"])\n    log(f\"Loaded {len(all_traces)} valid traces in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\")\n\n    _, _, test = split_traces(all_traces)\n    n_test = len(test)\n    log(f\"Test split: {n_test} traces\")\n    del all_traces\n    gc.collect()\n\n    num_layers = config[\"num_layers\"]\n    _HIDDEN_DIM = config[\"hidden_dim\"]\n\n    log(\"Precomputing layer data...\")\n    t_pre = time.time()\n    _LAYER_DATA = {}\n    for layer in range(num_layers):\n        _LAYER_DATA[layer] = [\n            (t[\"hidden_states\"][:, layer, :].float().numpy(), t[\"step_labels\"])\n            for t in test\n        ]\n    log(f\"Precomputed in {time.time()-t_pre:.1f}s\")\n    del test\n    gc.collect()\n\n    # Dry run (single process, single-threaded BLAS)\n    for w_test in [1, 5]:\n        t1 = time.time()\n        _, _, acc, _ = _worker_func((0, w_test))\n        dt = time.time() - t1\n        log(f\"Dry run L0/W={w_test}: acc={acc:.4f}, {dt:.1f}s\")\n\n    total_combos = num_layers * len(W_VALUES)\n    combos = [(layer, W) for layer in range(num_layers) for W in W_VALUES]\n    log(f\"Running {total_combos} combos with {N_WORKERS} processes...\")\n    t2 = time.time()\n\n    with Pool(processes=N_WORKERS) as pool:\n        results_raw = pool.map(_worker_func, combos, chunksize=1)\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t2\n    log(f\"All combos done in {elapsed:.1f}s ({elapsed/60:.1f} min)\")\n\n    results_raw.sort(key=lambda x: -x[2])\n\n    top5 = [{\"layer\": r[0], \"W\": r[1], \"loo_bal_acc\": round(r[2], 4)} for r in results_raw[:5]]\n\n    def find_entry(layer, W):\n        for rank, r in enumerate(results_raw, 1):\n            if r[0] == layer and r[1] == W:\n                return rank, round(r[2], 4)\n        return None, None\n\n    cb = config[\"current_biased\"]\n    cc = config[\"current_corrected\"]\n    b_rank, b_acc = find_entry(cb[\"layer\"], cb[\"W\"])\n    c_rank, c_acc = find_entry(cc[\"layer\"], cc[\"W\"])\n\n    log(f\"\\n--- {model_name} Results ---\")\n    log(f\"Top-5:\")\n    for i, t in enumerate(top5):\n        log(f\"  #{i+1}: L{t['layer']}, W={t['W']}, loo_bal_acc={t['loo_bal_acc']}\")\n    log(f\"Biased    L{cb['layer']}/W={cb['W']}: rank={b_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={b_acc}\")\n    log(f\"Corrected L{cc['layer']}/W={cc['W']}: rank={c_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={c_acc}\")\n\n    all_results_dict = {f\"L{r[0]}_W{r[1]}\": round(r[2], 4) for r in results_raw}\n\n    _LAYER_DATA = None\n    gc.collect()\n\n    return {\n        \"top5\": top5,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": cb[\"layer\"], \"W\": cb[\"W\"], \"rank\": b_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": b_acc},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": cc[\"layer\"], \"W\": cc[\"W\"], \"rank\": c_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": c_acc},\n        \"total_combinations\": total_combos,\n        \"n_test_traces\": n_test,\n        \"all_results\": all_results_dict,\n    }\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    with open(LOG, \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(f\"[{time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')}] Starting Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV\\n\")\n\n    results = {}\n    for model_name, config in MODEL_CONFIGS.items():\n        results[model_name] = run_model(model_name, config)\n\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"results.json\", \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n\n    log(\"\\nSaved to artifacts/exp_r9_joint_loo_cv/results.json\")\n    log(\"DONE\")\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"DONE\", \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(f\"completed at {time.strftime('%Y-%m-%d {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_0124jiLLGUAkxWKhUcxQTT1G","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01FGpniEqU1pBuNKX7Zp5nkC","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"autodl-rtx6000","command":"cat > /root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/scripts/run_joint_loo_cv.py << 'PYEOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"E-R9-3: Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV for QwQ-32B and R1-32B.\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nos.environ[\"OMP_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"1\"\nos.environ[\"MKL_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"1\"\nos.environ[\"OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"1\"\n\nimport json, time, gc\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.metrics import balanced_accuracy_score\nfrom multiprocessing import Pool\n\nSEED = 42\nBASE = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety\")\nOUTPUT_DIR = BASE / \"artifacts\" / \"exp_r9_joint_loo_cv\"\nOUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\nLOG = OUTPUT_DIR / \"run.log\"\n\nW_VALUES = [1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25]\nN_WORKERS = 200\n\nMODEL_CONFIGS = {\n    \"qwq_32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_qwq_32b\"),\n        \"num_layers\": 64,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": 2, \"W\": 5},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": 60, \"W\": 5},\n    },\n    \"r1_32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": str(BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_r1_32b\"),\n        \"num_layers\": 64,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": 10, \"W\": 10},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": 63, \"W\": 10},\n    },\n}\n\n_LAYER_DATA = None\n_HIDDEN_DIM = None\n\n\ndef log(msg):\n    ts = time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')\n    line = f\"[{ts}] {msg}\"\n    print(line, flush=True)\n    with open(LOG, \"a\") as f:\n        f.write(line + \"\\n\")\n\n\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\n    traces = []\n    for pt_file in sorted(Path(hs_dir).glob(\"*.pt\")):\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        if data.get(\"step_labels\") is None or data.get(\"commitment_point\") is None:\n            continue\n        cp = data[\"commitment_point\"]\n        labels = data[\"step_labels\"]\n        if cp <= 0 or cp >= len(labels) or sum(labels) == 0:\n            continue\n        traces.append(data)\n    return traces\n\n\ndef split_traces(traces, seed=SEED):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(traces)\n    idx = rng.permutation(n)\n    n_train = int(n * 0.6)\n    n_val = int(n * 0.2)\n    return ([traces[i] for i in idx[:n_train]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train:n_train + n_val]],\n            [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train + n_val:]])\n\n\ndef make_windowed_features(hs_np, labels, W, hidden_dim):\n    n = min(hs_np.shape[0], len(labels))\n    hs = hs_np[:n].astype(np.float32)\n    y = np.array(labels[:n], dtype=np.int32)\n    if W == 1:\n        return hs.copy(), y\n    half_w = W // 2\n    padded = np.zeros((n + W - 1, hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    padded[half_w:half_w + n] = hs\n    X = np.zeros((n, W * hidden_dim), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i in range(n):\n        X[i] = padded[i:i + W].ravel()\n    return X, y\n\n\ndef _worker_func(args):\n    layer, W = args\n    hidden_dim = _HIDDEN_DIM\n    trace_data = _LAYER_DATA[layer]\n    n = len(trace_data)\n\n    feats = [make_windowed_features(hs, lbl, W, hidden_dim) for hs, lbl in trace_data]\n\n    fold_scores = []\n    for i in range(n):\n        X_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][0] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n        y_tr = np.concatenate([feats[j][1] for j in range(n) if j != i])\n\n        if len(np.unique(y_tr)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        lr = LogisticRegression(solver='lbfgs', C=1.0, class_weight='balanced',\n                                max_iter=2000, random_state=SEED)\n        lr.fit(X_tr, y_tr)\n\n        X_te, y_te = feats[i]\n        if len(np.unique(y_te)) < 2:\n            fold_scores.append(0.5)\n            continue\n\n        pred = lr.predict(X_te)\n        fold_scores.append(float(balanced_accuracy_score(y_te, pred)))\n\n    return layer, W, float(np.mean(fold_scores)), fold_scores\n\n\ndef run_model(model_name, config):\n    global _LAYER_DATA, _HIDDEN_DIM\n    log(f\"=== {model_name} ===\")\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    all_traces = load_traces(config[\"hs_dir\"])\n    log(f\"Loaded {len(all_traces)} valid traces in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\")\n\n    _, _, test = split_traces(all_traces)\n    n_test = len(test)\n    log(f\"Test split: {n_test} traces\")\n    del all_traces\n    gc.collect()\n\n    num_layers = config[\"num_layers\"]\n    _HIDDEN_DIM = config[\"hidden_dim\"]\n\n    log(\"Precomputing layer data...\")\n    t_pre = time.time()\n    _LAYER_DATA = {}\n    for layer in range(num_layers):\n        _LAYER_DATA[layer] = [\n            (t[\"hidden_states\"][:, layer, :].float().numpy(), t[\"step_labels\"])\n            for t in test\n        ]\n    log(f\"Precomputed in {time.time()-t_pre:.1f}s\")\n    del test\n    gc.collect()\n\n    # Dry run (single process, single-threaded BLAS)\n    for w_test in [1, 5]:\n        t1 = time.time()\n        _, _, acc, _ = _worker_func((0, w_test))\n        dt = time.time() - t1\n        log(f\"Dry run L0/W={w_test}: acc={acc:.4f}, {dt:.1f}s\")\n\n    total_combos = num_layers * len(W_VALUES)\n    combos = [(layer, W) for layer in range(num_layers) for W in W_VALUES]\n    log(f\"Running {total_combos} combos with {N_WORKERS} fork processes...\")\n    t2 = time.time()\n\n    with Pool(processes=N_WORKERS) as pool:\n        results_raw = pool.map(_worker_func, combos, chunksize=1)\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t2\n    log(f\"All combos done in {elapsed:.1f}s ({elapsed/60:.1f} min)\")\n\n    results_raw = list(results_raw)\n    results_raw.sort(key=lambda x: -x[2])\n\n    top5 = [{\"layer\": r[0], \"W\": r[1], \"loo_bal_acc\": round(r[2], 4)} for r in results_raw[:5]]\n\n    def find_entry(layer, W):\n        for rank, r in enumerate(results_raw, 1):\n            if r[0] == layer and r[1] == W:\n                return rank, round(r[2], 4)\n        return None, None\n\n    cb = config[\"current_biased\"]\n    cc = config[\"current_corrected\"]\n    b_rank, b_acc = find_entry(cb[\"layer\"], cb[\"W\"])\n    c_rank, c_acc = find_entry(cc[\"layer\"], cc[\"W\"])\n\n    log(f\"\\n--- {model_name} Results ---\")\n    log(f\"Top-5:\")\n    for i, t in enumerate(top5):\n        log(f\"  #{i+1}: L{t['layer']}, W={t['W']}, loo_bal_acc={t['loo_bal_acc']}\")\n    log(f\"Biased    L{cb['layer']}/W={cb['W']}: rank={b_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={b_acc}\")\n    log(f\"Corrected L{cc['layer']}/W={cc['W']}: rank={c_rank}/{total_combos}, acc={c_acc}\")\n\n    all_results_dict = {f\"L{r[0]}_W{r[1]}\": round(r[2], 4) for r in results_raw}\n\n    _LAYER_DATA = None\n    gc.collect()\n\n    return {\n        \"top5\": top5,\n        \"current_biased\": {\"layer\": cb[\"layer\"], \"W\": cb[\"W\"], \"rank\": b_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": b_acc},\n        \"current_corrected\": {\"layer\": cc[\"layer\"], \"W\": cc[\"W\"], \"rank\": c_rank, \"loo_bal_acc\": c_acc},\n        \"total_combinations\": total_combos,\n        \"n_test_traces\": n_test,\n        \"all_results\": all_results_dict,\n    }\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    with open(LOG, \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(f\"[{time.strftime('%H:%M:%S')}] Starting Joint (layer, W) LOO-CV\\n\")\n\n    results = {}\n    for model_name, config in MODEL_CONFIGS.items():\n        results[model_name] = run_model(model_name, config)\n\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"results.json\", \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n\n    log(\"\\nSaved to artifacts/exp_r9_joint_loo_cv/results.json\")\n    log(\"DONE\")\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"DONE\", \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(f\"completed at {time.strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}\\n\")\nPYEOF\necho \"Script fixed.\"","account":"root"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":504,"cache_read_input_tokens":146919,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":504},"output_tokens":13,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b3423a64-4813-4c63-96b8-f04422b16989","uuid":"856a8d9b-0302-4dda-8713-1b18af05a287"}
